**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Lang's Testimony 3 states the president's body was hidden "inside that monster costume over there." However, Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Notes) indicates the body was found "lying face-up on top of the dirt" with "no dirt on the front of the body." This suggests the body was placed *on top* of the dirt, not *inside* the monster costume where dirt would be present.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction.

Prop 1 states the president's body is *inside* the monster costume, which aligns with Lang's testimony. However, Evidence 5 contradicts this by showing the body was *on top* of the dirt, implying it was not fully *inside* the costume where dirt would cover it.

**Step 3:** The contradiction arises because Prop 1 (body inside the costume) conflicts with Evidence 5 (body on top of the dirt with no dirt on the front). If the body were truly inside the costume, dirt would be on the front of the body, which is not the case.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning here is that the body's position (on top of the dirt) is inconsistent with being hidden *inside* the monster costume, as the costume's interior dirt would have transferred to the body.

{"evidence": 5, "testimony": 3}

--------------------
{
  "evidence": 5,
  "testimony": 3

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

The contradiction is not about spatial positioning but about the camera's purpose and triggering mechanism. However, we can examine the spatial propositions to see if they help clarify:

- Prop 2 mentions the Lake Photo shows a person holding a gun, which aligns with the camera being triggered by loud noise (gunshots)
- Prop 3 mentions fog between camera and people on lake, which is consistent with the Lake Photo
- Props 4 and 5 establish Lotta was in her car when the camera took the Lake Photo

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Looking at Simeon's testimonies, he claims he was in the lion hot air balloon when he saw the body double and Judge Gavèlle on the roof (Testimony 0 and 1). The body double then pointed a gun at him and fired (Testimony 3). However, the Bigg Building evidence (Evidence 2) states there is an entrance to the hidden fifty-first floor on the roof, which connects to the hatch mentioned in Testimony 4.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction.

Prop 3 states: "Simeon was in the lion hot air balloon above the rooftop when he saw the body double and Judge Gavèlle on the roof, therefore the balloon was directly above or near above the rooftop."

**Step 3:** The applicable proposition is Prop 3, which establishes that Simeon was above the rooftop in the balloon when the shooting occurred. However, this creates a spatial contradiction - if Simeon was above the rooftop in a balloon, and the body double was on the rooftop pointing a gun upward to fire at the balloon, this would be an extremely difficult angle for accurate shooting. More importantly, the testimonies don't mention the balloon being at rooftop level, but rather that Simeon was "riding the balloon to the rooftop," suggesting he was approaching rather than directly above when shot at.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that if Simeon was in a balloon approaching the rooftop (as suggested by "riding to the rooftop"), the body double couldn't have both been on the roof and able to fire accurately at a balloon that was still approaching rather than positioned directly above. This contradicts the implied spatial relationship in the testimonies.

{"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 4 reveals the contradiction because while the testimony claims the button was "ripped off" from the victim's costume during the struggle, the spatial proposition shows the button ended up inside Engarde's hakama. This creates a contradiction in the spatial relationship - if the button was truly ripped off during a struggle with the victim, it would more likely be found at the crime scene (near the victim's chair where Props 1, 2, and 3 are located), not concealed inside the defendant's clothing.

This contradiction is directly revealed by the spatial propositions. The cherry tree and candy stand are on opposite sides of the viewing platform (left vs right), making it impossible for Kay to see the pusher coming "from under the cherry tree" if they actually came from beyond the candy stand.

Proposition 5 clearly states that the coffee cup was found knocked over on the victim's table with coffee staining the tablecloth. This spatial arrangement makes it impossible for the victim to have picked up the cup and taken a sip as described in Kudo's testimony. The contradiction lies in Maya claiming this was Godot's "first time" arriving at the Inner Temple, when Prop 2 shows he must have been at the Main Temple before crossing to the Inner Temple after the bridge was fixed.

Propositions 1, 2, and 3 directly apply to this contradiction. According to Prop 1 and 2, Valerie's body is in the car trunk located in the parking lot west of Dusky Bridge. According to Prop 3, Fawles is on Dusky Bridge. These propositions establish that Valerie's body and Fawles are in two separate locations - the parking lot and the bridge respectively. The contradiction is directly revealed by examining Prop 2 and Prop 4 together.

Prop 2 indicates the shot was fired FROM the lounge TOWARD the Control Room (where Cosmos and Arme were), but Prop 4 indicates the bullet that killed Terran traveled FROM the Control Room direction TOWARD the lounge. - Near side (defendant's side): 4 red small chips (400 points) + 1 gray big chip (1,000 points) = 1,400 points
- Far side (victim's side): 2 red small chips (200 points) + 9 gray big chips (9,000 points) = 9,200 points

This spatial arrangement (Prop 4 - cards and chips on the table) shows the victim had significantly more chips (9,200 vs 1,400), which would indicate the victim was winning. However, this contradicts Testimony 4 which states "For last hand, defendant play with all chips on table and lose." If the defendant had only 1,400 points and the victim had 9,200, the defendant couldn't have been playing with "all chips on table" as the victim's chips weren't at risk.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in the spatial arrangement of chips shown in Evidence 6 (Chip Photo) versus the claim in Testimony 3 that the victim was winning. The photo shows the victim had overwhelming chip advantage (9,200 vs 1,400), making it impossible for the defendant to have been "playing with all chips on table" as claimed in Testimony 4.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 3}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 6,
  "testimony": 3
}

The contradiction lies in Lance's spatial awareness. If he was truly blindfolded the entire time (Testimony 1), he would have no visual confirmation that the door was locked. Yet in Testimony 5, he confidently states the door was "locked tight" as his reason for choosing the underground passageway. This suggests either:
1. He wasn't actually blindfolded the entire time, or
2. He had prior knowledge of the door's state

This directly contradicts his claim of being blindfolded throughout his captivity.

- Testimony 1: She was searching for Yatagarasu in Babahl during the second fire
- Testimony 2: She heard a scream from the next room over while searching
- Testimony 3: She was in the next room but couldn't see Yatagarasu

Looking at the evidence:
- Evidence 29: The revolving fireplace wall in Babahl was used, and Yatagarasu escaped through it into the next room over

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 1: If Shih-na was in the next room over from where Yatagarasu appeared, then Yatagarasu was in a room adjacent to Shih-na's location.
Prop 2: If Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into the next room over, then Yatagarasu moved from inside the fireplace room to inside the adjacent room.
Prop 3: If Shih-na was searching for Yatagarasu in Babahl during the second fire, then Shih-na was inside Babahl's embassy section when Yatagarasu was spotted.
Prop 5: If Shih-na heard a scream coming from the next room over while searching, then the scream originated from a room adjacent to Shih-na's position at that time.

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

From Testimony 1 and Prop 3: Shih-na was inside Babahl's embassy section when Yatagarasu was spotted.
From Evidence 29 and Prop 2: Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into the next room over, meaning Yatagarasu moved from the fireplace room to the adjacent room.

This creates a contradiction because if Yatagarasu escaped into the next room (Evidence 29), and Shih-na was in that next room (Testimony 3), then she should have been able to see Yatagarasu entering that room. The spatial arrangement suggests these two positions should have overlapped, making it impossible for her to be in the next room without seeing Yatagarasu.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is revealed through spatial reasoning

The evidence shows Yatagarasu escaped into the next room over (Evidence 29), while Shih-na claims she was in that same next room but didn't see Yatagarasu (Testimony 3). These two statements cannot both be true simultaneously given the spatial relationship described.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

The gunpowder burn evidence proves the gun was very close to the victim when fired, which contradicts the claim that the shot came from above the stage.

- Testimony 2: Claims he saw coins scattered around the pool room from the doorway

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 1 states: "If Rimes was in the staff room at 10:10 AM and the orca pool room is in a different location from the staff room, then Rimes was not in the orca pool room at 10:10 AM."

**Step 3:** Apply the relevant proposition

According to Prop 1, if Rimes was indeed in the staff room at 10:10 AM (as per Testimony 0), and the orca pool room is in a different location (as indicated by the spatial layout), then Rimes could not have been at the orca pool room door at 10:10 AM to see the coins scattered around. This directly contradicts Testimonies 1 and 2 where he claims to have seen the coins from the pool room doorway.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is revealed through spatial reasoning

The spatial impossibility lies in Rimes claiming to be in the staff room at 10:10 AM while simultaneously claiming to be at the orca pool room door observing the crime scene. These two locations are separate, making his simultaneous presence at both impossible.

The fact that the victim's face is obscured by a hat in the photograph suggests the photo was taken from an angle consistent with someone looking from the front of the omnibus toward the back. This directly contradicts Beppo's claim that he "didn't actually see anything" - his position as driver would have given him a clear line of sight to the victim.
